It takes its revenue through a [progressive income tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Progressive_tax), a 9.975% sales tax,[^cite_note-184] various other provincial taxes (ex. carbon, corporate and capital gains taxes), [equalization payments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Equalization_payments_in_Canada), transfer payments from other provinces, and direct payments.[^cite_note-185] By some measures Quebec residents are the most taxed;[^cite_note-186] a 2012 study indicated that "Quebec companies pay 26 per cent more in taxes than the Canadian average".[^cite_note-187]

Quebec's immigration philosophy is based on the principles of pluralism and [interculturalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interculturalism).The *Ministère de l'Immigration et des Communautés culturelles du Québec* is responsible for the selection and integration of immigrants.[^cite_note-188] Programs favour immigrants who know French, have a low risk of becoming criminals and have in-demand skills.

Quebec's health and social services network is administered by the [Ministry of Health and Social Services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Health_and_Social_Services_\(Quebec\)). It is composed of 95 *réseaux locaux de services* (RLS; 'local service networks') and 18 *agences de la santé et des services sociaux* (ASSS; 'health and social services agencies'). Quebec's health system is supported by the *[Régie de l'assurance maladie du Québec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R%C3%A9gie_de_l'assurance_maladie_du_Qu%C3%A9bec)* (RAMQ) which works to maintain the accessibility of services for all citizens of Quebec.[^cite_note-189]

The *Ministère de la Famille et des Aînés du Québec* operate [centres de la petite enfance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centres_de_la_petite_enfance?action=edit\&redlink=1) (CPEs; 'centres for young children'). Quebec [provides universal low-fee childcare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Child_care_in_Canada#Child_care_in_Qu%C3%A9bec) for all children under 12. [Quebec's education system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Quebec) is administered by the [Ministry of Education and Higher Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Education_and_Higher_Education_\(Quebec\)) ([primary](25058.md) and [secondary](554992.md) schools), the *Ministère de l'Enseignement supérieur* ([CEGEP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CEGEP)) and the *Conseil supérieure de l'Education du Québec* (universities and colleges).[^cite_note-190] In 2012, the annual cost for postsecondary [tuition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tuition_payments) was CA\$2,168 (€1,700) – less than half of Canada's average tuition. Part of the reason for this is that tuition fees were frozen to a relatively low level when CEGEPS were created during the Quiet Revolution. When Jean Charest's government decided in 2012 to sharply increase university fees, [students protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Quebec_student_protests) erupted.[^cite_note-191] Because of these protests, Quebec's tuition fees remain relatively low.

### External relationships

Quebec's closest international partner is the United States, with which it shares a long and positive history. Products of [American culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_culture) like songs, movies, fashion and food strongly affect Québécois culture.

Quebec has a historied relationship with France, as Quebec was a part of the French Empire and both regions share a language. The [Fédération France-Québec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F%C3%A9d%C3%A9ration_France-Qu%C3%A9bec?action=edit\&redlink=1) and the [Francophonie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francophonie) are a few of the tools used for relations between Quebec and France. In Paris, a *place du Québec* was inaugurated in 1980.[^cite_note-192] Quebec also has a historied relationship with the United Kingdom, having been a part of the British Empire. Quebec and the UK share the same head of state, King [Charles III](125248.md).

Quebec has a [network of 32 offices](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quebec_Government_Offices) in 18 countries. These offices serve the purpose of representing Quebec in foreign countries and are overseen by Quebec's [Ministry of International Relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_International_Relations_\(Quebec\)). Quebec, like other Canadian provinces, also maintains representatives in some Canadian embassies and consulates general. As of 2019, the Government of Quebec had delegates-general ([agents-general](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agents-general)) in [Brussels](3708.md), London, Mexico City, [Munich](19058.md), New York City, Paris and Tokyo; delegates to [Atlanta](3138.md), [Boston](24437894.md), Chicago, [Houston](13774.md), Los Angeles, and Rome; and offices headed by directors offering more limited services in [Barcelona](4443.md), Beijing, [Dakar](44251.md), [Hong Kong](13404.md), [Mumbai](19189.md), [São Paulo](390875.md), [Shanghai](27643.md), [Stockholm](26741.md), and [Washington](108956.md). In addition, there are the equivalent of [honorary consuls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consul_\(representative\)#honorary_consul), titled *antennes*, in [Berlin](3354.md), [Philadelphia](50585.md), [Qingdao](250171.md), [Seoul](19159283.md), and [Silicon Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silicon_Valley).

Quebec also has a representative to [UNESCO](21786641.md) and participates in the [Organization of American States](22788.md).[^cite_note-193] Quebec is a member of the [Assemblée parlementaire de la Francophonie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assembl%C3%A9e_parlementaire_de_la_Francophonie) and of the [Organisation internationale de la francophonie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organisation_internationale_de_la_francophonie).

## Law

Main articles: [Quebec law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quebec_law) and [Bar of Quebec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bar_of_Quebec)



The [Édifice Ernest-Cormier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89difice_Ernest-Cormier) is the courthouse for the [Quebec Court of Appeal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quebec_Court_of_Appeal) in Montreal.

Quebec law is the shared responsibility of the [federal](616255.md) and [provincial government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Quebec). The federal government is responsible for [criminal law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Criminal_law), foreign affairs and laws relating to the regulation of Canadian commerce, interprovincial transport, and telecommunications.[^cite_note-CF-194] The provincial government is responsible for [private law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Private_law), the administration of justice, and several social domains, such as social assistance, healthcare, education, and natural resources.[^cite_note-CF-194]

Quebec law is influenced by two judicial traditions ([civil law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_law_\(legal_system\)) and [common law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Common_law)) and four classic [sources of law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sources_of_law) ([legislation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legislation), [case law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Case_law), doctrine and [customary law](218798.md)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKélada197021-195] Private law in Quebec affects all relationships between individuals ([natural](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_person) or [juridical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juridical_person) persons) and is largely under the jurisdiction of the [Parliament of Quebec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_of_Quebec). The [Parliament of Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_of_Canada) also influences Quebec private law, in particular through its power over banks, bankruptcy, marriage, divorce and [maritime law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maritime_law).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrunTremblayBrouillet2008474–491-196] The *[Droit civil du Québec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Droit_civil_du_Qu%C3%A9bec?action=edit\&redlink=1)* is the primary component of Quebec's private law and is [codified](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Codification_\(law\)) in the [Civil Code of Quebec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_Code_of_Quebec).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEÉmondLauzière200338-197] Public law in Quebec is largely derived from the common law tradition.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrunTremblayBrouillet20089,_28-198] Quebec [constitutional law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_law) governs the rules surrounding the Quebec government, the Parliament of Quebec and Quebec's courts. Quebec [administrative law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Administrative_law) governs relations between individuals and the Quebec public administration. Quebec also has some limited jurisdiction over criminal law. Finally, Quebec, like the federal government, has [tax law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tax_law) power.[^cite_note-199] Certain portions of Quebec law are considered mixed. This is the case, for example, with [human rights and freedoms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights_and_freedoms) which are governed by the [Quebec Charter of Human Rights and Freedoms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quebec_Charter_of_Human_Rights_and_Freedoms), a Charter which applies to both government and citizens.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEÉmondLauzière200339–40-200][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrunTremblayBrouillet2008943–945-201]

English is not an official language in [Quebec law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quebec_law).[^cite_note-charteroffrench2-202] However, both English and French are required by the [Constitution Act, 1867](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_Act,_1867) for the enactment of laws and regulations, and any person may use English or French in the National Assembly and the courts. The books and records of the National Assembly must also be kept in both languages.[^cite_note-203][^cite_note-204]

### Courts

Although Quebec is a civil law jurisdiction, it does not follow the pattern of other civil law systems which have court systems divided by subject matter. Instead, the court system follows the English model of unitary courts of general jurisdiction. The provincial courts have jurisdiction to decide matters under [provincial law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Law_of_Canada) as well as [federal law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_law), including [civil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_law_\(legal_system\)), [criminal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Criminal_law) and [constitutional matters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_law).[^cite_note-205] The major exception to the principle of general jurisdiction is that the [Federal Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Court_\(Canada\)) and [Federal Court of Appeal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Court_of_Appeal) have exclusive jurisdiction over some areas of federal law, such as review of federal administrative bodies, federal taxes, and matters relating to national security.[^cite_note-206]

The Quebec courts are organized in a pyramid. At the bottom, there are the [municipal courts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipal_court), the Professions Tribunal, the Human Rights Tribunal, and administrative tribunals. Decisions of those bodies can be reviewed by the two trial courts, the [Court of Quebec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Court_of_Quebec) the [Superior Court of Quebec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superior_Court_of_Quebec). The Court of Quebec is the main criminal trial court, and also a court for small civil claims. The Superior Court is a trial court of general jurisdiction, in both criminal and civil matters. The decisions of those courts can be appealed to the [Quebec Court of Appeal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quebec_Court_of_Appeal). Finally, if the case is of great importance, it may be appealed to the [Supreme Court of Canada](123468.md).

The Court of Appeal serves two purposes. First, it is the general court of appeal for all legal issues from the lower courts. It hears appeals from the trial decisions of the Superior Court and the Quebec Court. It also can hear appeals from decisions rendered by those two courts on appeals or judicial review matters relating to the municipal courts and administrative tribunals.[^cite_note-207] Second, but much more rarely, the Court of Appeal possesses the power to respond to [reference questions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reference_question) posed to it by the [Quebec Cabinet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Executive_Council_of_Quebec). The Court of Appeal renders more than 1,500 judgments per year.[^cite_note-208]

### Law enforcement

The [Sûreté du Québec](375500.md) is the main police force of Quebec. The Sûreté du Québec can also serve a support and coordination role with other police forces, such as with municipal police forces or with the [Royal Canadian Mounted Police](69080.md) (RCMP).[^cite_note-209][^cite_note-210] The RCMP has the power to enforce certain federal laws in Quebec. However, given the existence of the Sûreté du Québec, its role is more limited than in the other provinces.[^cite_note-211]

Municipal police, such as the [Service de police de la Ville de Montréal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Service_de_police_de_la_Ville_de_Montr%C3%A9al) and the [Service de police de la Ville de Québec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Service_de_police_de_la_Ville_de_Qu%C3%A9bec), are responsible for law enforcement in their municipalities. The Sûreté du Québec fulfils the role of municipal police in the 1038 municipalities that do not have a municipal police force.[^cite_note-212] The Indigenous communities of Quebec have their own police forces.[^cite_note-213]

For offences against provincial or federal laws in Quebec (including the [Criminal Code](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Criminal_Code_\(Canada\))), the [Director of Criminal and Penal Prosecutions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Director_of_Criminal_and_Penal_Prosecutions) is responsible for prosecuting offenders in court through [Crown attorneys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crown_attorney). The [Department of Justice of Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Justice_\(Canada\)) also has the power to prosecute offenders, but only for offences against specific federal laws (ex. selling [narcotics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Narcotic)). Quebec is responsible for operating the [prison system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Public_Security_\(Quebec\)) for sentences of less than two years, and the federal government operates [penitentiaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Incarceration_in_Canada) for sentences of two years or more.[^cite_note-214]

## Demographics

Main articles: [Demographics of Quebec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Quebec) and [Demographic history of Quebec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographic_history_of_Quebec)



Population density map of Quebec

In the [2021 census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Canadian_census), Quebec's population was determined to be 8,501,833, a 4.1% increase from its 2016 population of 8,164,361. With a land area of 1,356,625.27 km<sup>2</sup> (523,795.95 sq mi), it had a population density of 6.0/km<sup>2</sup> (15.6/sq mi) in 2016. Quebec accounted for a little under 23% of the Canadian population. The [largest cities in Quebec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_population_centres_in_Quebec) are [Montreal](7954681.md) (1,762,976), [Quebec City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quebec_City) (538,738), [Laval](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laval,_Quebec) (431,208), and [Gatineau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gatineau,_Quebec) (281,501).[^cite_note-215]

In 2016, Quebec's median age was 41.2 years. As of 2020, 20.8% of the population was younger than 20, 59.5% was aged between 20 and 64, and 19.7% was 65 or older. In 2019, Quebec witnessed an increase in the number of births compared to the year before (84,200 vs 83,840) and had a [total fertility rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Total_fertility_rate) of about 1.6 children born per woman. As of 2020, the average life expectancy was 82.3 years. Quebec in 2019 registered its highest rate of population growth since 1972, with an increase of 110,000 people, mostly because of the arrival of a high number of immigrants. As of 2019, most international immigrants were from China, India and France.[^cite_note-216] In 2016, 30% of the population possessed a [postsecondary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Postsecondary) degree or diploma. Most residents, particularly couples, are [property owners](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Property_owner). In 2016, 80% of both property owners and renters considered their housing to be "unaffordable".[^cite_note-habitation-217] In the [2021 Canadian census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Canadian_census), 29.3% of Quebec's population stated their ancestry was of Canadian origin and 21.1% stated their ancestry was of French origin.[^cite_note-218] As of 2021, 18% of Quebec's population belonged to a [visible minority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visible_minorities) group.[^cite_note-219]

### Religion

Main article: [Religion in Quebec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_Quebec)

Religion in Quebec ([2021 census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Canadian_census))

[^cite_note-2021censusB-220]

1. [Christianity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity_in_Canada) (64.8%)

2. [No religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irreligion_in_Canada) (27.3%)

3. [Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_Canada) (5.10%)

4. [Judaism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judaism_in_Canada) (1.00%)

5. [Buddhism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhism_in_Canada) (0.60%)

6. [Hinduism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism_in_Canada) (0.60%)

7. [Sikhism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sikhism_in_Canada) (0.30%)

8. Other / Not stated (0.30%)



The [Basilica of Sainte-Anne-de-Beaupré](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basilica_of_Sainte-Anne-de-Beaupr%C3%A9).

According to the [2021 census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Canadian_census), the most commonly cited religions in Quebec were:[^cite_note-2021censusB-220]

- [Christianity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity_in_Canada) (5,385,240 residents, or 64.8%)

- [Irreligion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irreligion_in_Canada) (2,267,720 or 27.3%)

- [Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_Canada) (421,710 or 5.1%)

- [Judaism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judaism_in_Canada) (84,530 or 1.0%)

- [Buddhism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhism_in_Canada) (48,365 or 0.6%)

- [Hinduism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism_in_Canada) (47,390 or 0.6%)

- [Sikhism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sikhism_in_Canada) (23,345 or 0.3%)

- [Indigenous spirituality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mythologies_of_the_indigenous_peoples_of_the_Americas) (3,790 or <0.1%)

- Other (26,385 or 0.3%)

The Roman Catholic Church has long occupied a central and integral place in Quebec society since the foundation of Quebec City in 1608. However, since the [Quiet Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quiet_Revolution), which secularized Quebec, irreligion has been growing significantly.[^cite_note-221] In 2025, there was a reported rise in adult baptisms and an increased number of baptisms of people over the age of 7.[^cite_note-222] The Catholic Church of Quebec described the recent increase in baptisms among adults and adolescents as a "surprising phenomenon … gaining momentum" and that interest in Catholic baptism is "especially present" in young people and in adults from immigrant backgrounds.[^cite_note-223] Later on [Holy Saturday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holy_Saturday) in 2026, it was reported that young people were joining the Catholic Church due to the presence of priests and young influencers on social media platforms such as [TikTok](56822861.md) and [Instagram](31591547.md).[^cite_note-224] Other reasons for young people joining the Catholic Church was reported to be due to taking an interest in Quebec's religious heritage so that they could "understand their identity."[^cite_note-225]

Religions other than Christianity, Judaism and indigenous faiths were not present in Quebec before the 20th century. They started establishing a small presence following the passing of the *Immigration Act of 1962*. Islam in particular has grown rapidly since the 1990s due to high immigration levels. Its number of adherents increased from 44,930 (0.6% of the population) in 1991 to 421,715 (5.1%) in 2021.[^cite_note-226][^cite_note-227]

The oldest [parish church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parish_church) in North America is the [Cathedral-Basilica of Notre-Dame de Québec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cathedral-Basilica_of_Notre-Dame_de_Qu%C3%A9bec). Its construction began in 1647, when it was known under the name *Notre-Dame-de-la-Paix*, and it was finished in 1664.[^cite_note-228] The most frequented place of worship in Quebec is the [Basilica of Sainte-Anne-de-Beaupré](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basilica_of_Sainte-Anne-de-Beaupr%C3%A9). This basilica welcomes millions of visitors each year. [Saint Joseph's Oratory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Joseph's_Oratory) is the largest place of worship in the world dedicated to [Saint Joseph](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Joseph). Many pilgrimages include places such as [Saint Benedict Abbey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Benedict_Abbey,_Quebec), [Sanctuaire Notre-Dame-du-Cap](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanctuaire_Notre-Dame-du-Cap?action=edit\&redlink=1), [Notre-Dame de Montréal Basilica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Notre-Dame_de_Montr%C3%A9al_Basilica), [Marie-Reine-du-Monde de Montréal Basilica-Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mary,_Queen_of_the_World_Cathedral), [Saint-Michel Basilica-Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint-Michel_Basilica-Cathedral), and [Saint-Patrick's Basilica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Patrick's_Basilica,_Montreal). Another important place of worship in Quebec is the Anglican [Holy Trinity Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cathedral_of_the_Holy_Trinity_\(Quebec\)), which was erected between 1800 and 1804. It was the first Anglican cathedral built outside the British Isles.[^cite_note-229]

### Language

Main article: [Language demographics of Quebec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Language_demographics_of_Quebec)

See also: [French language in Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_language_in_Canada)



| Linguistic map of the province of Quebec (source: Statistics Canada, 2006 census)   Francophone majority, less than 33% Anglophone  Francophone majority, more than 33% Anglophone  Anglophone majority, more than 33% Francophone  Anglophone majority, less than 33% Francophone  Indigenous language majority  Data not available |
| --- |

Quebec differs from other Canadian provinces in that French is its sole [official](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Official_language) language, while English predominates in the [rest of Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rest_of_Canada).[^cite_note-230] French is the [common language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Common_language), understood and spoken by 93.7% of the population according to the [2021 census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Canadian_census),[^cite_note-language2021-231] and is the sole native language of 74.8% of the population (or slightly more than 6.5 million residents) and a native language (alone or in combination with others) of 77.8%.[^cite_note-TP-232] This makes Quebec the only Canadian province whose population is mainly Francophone. [Quebec French](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quebec_French) is the umbrella term for local [variants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Variation_\(linguistics\)) of the language. Canada is estimated to be home to roughly 30 regional French accents,[^cite_note-quebeccultureblog.com2-233][^cite_note-234] 17 of which can be found in Quebec.[^cite_note-235] 42.2% of Quebec's population with a French mother tongue can converse in English, the predominant language of the rest of Canada.[^cite_note-236]

The [Office québécois de la langue française](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Office_qu%C3%A9b%C3%A9cois_de_la_langue_fran%C3%A7aise) oversees the application of linguistic policies respecting French on the territory, jointly with the [Superior Council of the French Language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superior_Council_of_the_French_Language_\(Quebec\)) and the [Commission de toponymie du Québec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commission_de_toponymie_du_Qu%C3%A9bec). The foundation for these linguistic policies was created in 1968 by the [Gendron Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commission_of_Inquiry_on_the_Situation_of_the_French_Language_and_Linguistic_Rights_in_Quebec) and they have been accompanied the [Charter of the French language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charter_of_the_French_language) ("Bill 101") since 1977. The policies are in effect to protect Quebec from being assimilated by its English-speaking neighbours (the rest of Canada and the United States)[^cite_note-237][^cite_note-238] and were also created to rectify historical injustice between the Francophone majority and Anglophone minority, the latter of which were favoured since Quebec was a colony of the British Empire.[^cite_note-239] Quebec remains, alongside [Haiti](13373.md), the only major Francophone dominant regions in the Americas.[^cite_note-240][^cite_note-241][^cite_note-242]

[Anglo-Quebecers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anglo-Quebecers), a name for residents whose main language is English, constitute the second largest linguistic group in Quebec. In 2021, English was the sole mother tongue of 7.6% of Quebec residents, and was a native language (alone or in combination with others) of 10.0%.[^cite_note-TP-232] Anglo-Quebecers reside mainly in the west of the island of Montreal ([West Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Island)), [downtown Montreal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downtown_Montreal) and the [Pontiac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pontiac_Regional_County_Municipality).

Three families of Indigenous languages encompassing eleven languages exist in Quebec: the [Algonquian language family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algonquian_languages) ([Abenaki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abenaki_language), [Algonquin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algonquin_language), [Maliseet-passamaquoddy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malecite-Passamaquoddy_language), [Mi'kmaq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mi'kmaq_language), and the [linguistic continuum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linguistic_continuum) of [Atikamekw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atikamekw_language), [Cree](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cree_language), [Innu-aimun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Innu_language), and [Naskapi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naskapi_language)), the [Inuit–Aleut language family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eskimo%E2%80%93Aleut_languages) (Nunavimmiutitut, an [Inuktitut dialect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inuit_grammar) spoken by the [Inuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inuit) of [Nord-du-Québec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nord-du-Qu%C3%A9bec)), and the [Iroquoian language family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iroquoian_languages) ([Mohawk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohawk_language) and [Wendat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wendat_language)). In the 2016 census, 50,895 people said they knew at least one Indigenous language[^cite_note-243] and 45,570 people declared having an Indigenous language as their mother tongue.[^cite_note-244] In Quebec, most Indigenous languages are transmitted quite well from one generation to the next with a mother tongue retention rate of 92%.[^cite_note-245]

As of the [2016 census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Canadian_census), the most common immigrant languages claimed as a native language were Arabic (2.5% of the total population), Spanish (1.9%), Italian (1.4%), Creole languages (mainly [Haitian Creole](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haitian_Creole)) (0.8%), and Mandarin (0.6%).[^cite_note-246]

As of the [2021 Canadian census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Canadian_census), the ten most spoken languages in the province were French (spoken by 7,786,735 people, or 93.72% of the population), English (4,317,180 or 51.96%), Spanish (453,905 or 5.46%), Arabic (343,675 or 4.14%), Italian (168,040 or 2.02%), Haitian Creole (118,010 or 1.42%), Mandarin (80,520 or 0.97%), Portuguese (65,605 or 0.8%), Russian (55,485 or 0.7%), and Greek (50,375 or 0.6%).[^cite_note-language2021-231] The question on knowledge of languages allows for multiple responses.

### Indigenous peoples

Main article: [Indigenous peoples in Quebec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indigenous_peoples_in_Quebec)



Map of aboriginal communities in Quebec, this includes [reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_reserve), [settlements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_settlement) and [northern villages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Types_of_municipalities_in_Quebec#Aboriginal_local_municipal_units):&#x20;

&#x20; Algonquin

&#x20; Attikameks

&#x20; Abenaki

&#x20; Cree

&#x20; Wendat

&#x20; Innu

&#x20; Wolastoqiyik

&#x20; Mi'kmaq

&#x20; Mohawk

&#x20; Naskapi

&#x20; Inuit

In 2021, the Indigenous population of Quebec numbered 205,010 (2.5% of the population), including 15,800 [Inuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inuit), 116,550 [First Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Nations_in_Canada) people, and 61,010 [Métis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C3%A9tis).[^cite_note-247] There is an undercount, as some Indian bands regularly refuse to participate in Canadian censuses. In 2016, the [Mohawk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohawk_people) reserves of [Kahnawake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kahnawake) and [Doncaster 17](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doncaster,_Quebec) along with the [Indian settlement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_settlement) of [Kanesatake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanesatake) and [Lac-Rapide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rapid_Lake), a reserve of the [Algonquins of Barriere Lake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algonquins_of_Barriere_Lake), were not counted.[^cite_note-248]

The Inuit of Quebec live mainly in [Nunavik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nunavik) in [Nord-du-Québec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nord-du-Qu%C3%A9bec). They make up the majority of the population living north of the 55th parallel. There are ten First Nations ethnic groups in Quebec: the [Abenaki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abenaki), the [Algonquin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algonquin_people), the [Attikamek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attikamek), the [Cree](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cree), the [Wolastoqiyik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wolastoqiyik), the [Mi'kmaq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mi'kmaq), the [Innu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Innu), the [Naskapi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naskapi), the [Wendat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wendat_Nation) and the [Mohawk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohawks). The Mohawks were once part of the [Iroquois Confederacy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iroquois_Confederacy). Aboriginal rights were enunciated in the [Indian Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Act) and adopted at the end of the 19th century. This act confines [First Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Nations_in_Canada) within the [reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_reserve) created for them. The Indian Act is still in effect today.[^cite_note-249] In 1975, the Cree, [Inuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inuit) and the Quebec government agreed to an agreement called the [James Bay and Northern Quebec Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Bay_and_Northern_Quebec_Agreement) that would extended indigenous rights beyond reserves, and to over two-thirds of Quebec's territory. Because this extension was enacted without the participation of the [federal government](616255.md), the extended indigenous rights only exist in Quebec. In 1978, the Naskapis joined the agreement when the [Northeastern Quebec Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northeastern_Quebec_Agreement) was signed. Discussions have been underway with the Innu of the [Côte-Nord](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C%C3%B4te-Nord) and [Saguenay–Lac-Saint-Jean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saguenay%E2%80%93Lac-Saint-Jean) for the potential creation of a similar autonomy in two new distinct territories that would be called *Innu Assi* and *Nitassinan*.[^cite_note-250]

A few political institutions have also been created over time:

- The [Assembly of First Nations Quebec-Labrador](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assembly_of_First_Nations_Quebec-Labrador)

  [^cite_note-251]

- The [Grand Council of the Crees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Council_of_the_Crees)

  [^cite_note-252]

- The [Makivik Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Makivik_Corporation)

  [^cite_note-253]

### Acadians

The subject of [Acadians in Quebec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acadians_in_Quebec) is an important one as more than a million people in Quebec are of [Acadian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acadians) descent, with roughly 4.8 million people possessing one or multiple Acadian ancestors in their genealogy tree, because a large number of Acadians had fled [Acadia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acadia) to take refuge in Quebec during the [Great Upheaval](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Upheaval). Furthermore, more than a million people have a [patronym](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patronym) of Acadian origin.[^cite_note-254][^cite_note-255][^cite_note-256][^cite_note-257]

Quebec houses Acadian communities. Acadians mainly live on the [Magdalen Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magdalen_Islands) and in [Gaspesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaspesia), but about thirty other communities are present elsewhere in Quebec, mostly in the Côte-Nord and [Centre-du-Québec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centre-du-Qu%C3%A9bec) regions. An Acadian community in Quebec can be called a "Cadie", "Petite Cadie" or "Cadien".[^cite_note-258]

## Economy

Main article: [Economy of Quebec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Quebec)

Quebec has an [advanced](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Developed_country), [market-based](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Market_economy), and [open economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open_economy). In 2022, its [gross domestic product](12594.md) (GDP) was [US\$50,000](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Canadian_provinces_and_territories_by_gross_domestic_product) per person at [purchasing power parity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purchasing_power_parity).[^cite_note-259] The economy of Quebec is the [46th largest in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_\(PPP\)) behind [Chile](5489.md) and [29th](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_\(PPP\)_per_capita) for GDP per person.[^cite_note-260][^cite_note-261] Quebec represents 19% of the GDP of Canada. The provincial [debt-to-GDP ratio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Debt-to-GDP_ratio) peaked at 51% in 2012–2013, and declined to 43% in 2021.[^cite_note-262]

Like most industrialized countries, the economy is based mainly on the [services sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Services_sector). Quebec's economy has traditionally been fuelled by abundant natural resources and a well-developed infrastructure, but has undergone significant change over the past decade.[^cite_note-263] Firmly grounded in the [knowledge economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Knowledge_economy), Quebec has one of the highest growth rates of GDP in Canada. The knowledge sector represents about 31% of Quebec's GDP.[^cite_note-264] In 2011, Quebec experienced faster growth of its [research-and-development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Research_and_development) (R\&D) spending than other Canadian provinces.[^cite_note-265] Quebec's spending in R\&D in 2011 was equal to 2.63% of GDP, above the [European Union](9317.md) average of 1.8%.[^cite_note-266] The percentage spent on research and technology is the highest in Canada and higher than the averages for the [Organisation for Economic Co-operation and Development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organisation_for_Economic_Co-operation_and_Development) and the [G7](1196634.md) countries.[^cite_note-267]

Some of the most important companies from Quebec are: [Bombardier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombardier_Inc), [Desjardins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Desjardins_Group), the [National Bank of Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Bank_of_Canada), the [Jean Coutu Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean_Coutu_Group), [Transcontinental média](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transcontinental_\(company\)), [Quebecor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quebecor), the [Métro Inc.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metro_Inc.) food retailers, [Hydro-Québec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hydro-Qu%C3%A9bec), the [Société des alcools du Québec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soci%C3%A9t%C3%A9_des_alcools_du_Qu%C3%A9bec), the [Bank of Montreal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bank_of_Montreal), [Saputo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saputo_Inc), the [Cirque du Soleil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cirque_du_Soleil), the [Caisse de dépôt et placement du Québec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caisse_de_d%C3%A9p%C3%B4t_et_placement_du_Qu%C3%A9bec), the Normandin restaurants, and [Vidéotron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vid%C3%A9otron).

### Exports and imports



Quebec's exports to the international market. The United States is the country which buys the most exports from Quebec by far. (2011)

Thanks to the [World Trade Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Organization) (WTO) and the [North American Free Trade Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_American_Free_Trade_Agreement) (NAFTA), Quebec had, as of 2009, experienced an increase in its exports and in its ability to compete on the international market. International exchanges contribute to the strength of the Quebec economy.[^cite_note-PQ-268] NAFTA is especially advantageous as it gives Quebec, among other things, access to a market of 130 million consumers within a radius of 1,000 kilometres.

In 2008, Quebec's exports to other provinces in Canada and abroad totalled 157.3 billion CND\$, or 51.8% of Quebec's gross domestic product (GDP). Of this total, 60.4% were international exports, and 39.6% were interprovincial exports. The breakdown by destination of international merchandise exports is: United States (72.2%), Europe (14.4%), Asia (5.1%), Middle East (2.7%), Central America (2.3%), South America (1.9%), Africa (0.8%) and Oceania (0.7%).[^cite_note-PQ-268]

In 2008, Quebec imported \$178 billion worth of goods and services, or 58.6% of its GDP. Of this total, 62.9% of goods were imported from international markets, while 37.1% of goods were interprovincial imports. The breakdown by origin of international merchandise imports is as follows: United States (31.1%), Europe (28.7%), Asia (17.1%), Africa (11.7%), South America (4.5%), Central America (3.7%), Middle East (1.3%) and Oceania (0.7%).[^cite_note-PQ-268]

### Primary sector



The [Beauharnois generating station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beauharnois_generating_station), operated by [Hydro-Québec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hydro-Qu%C3%A9bec)

Quebec produces most of Canada's [hydroelectricity](381399.md) and is the second biggest hydroelectricity producer in the world (2019).[^cite_note-269] Because of this, Quebec has been described as a potential [clean energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clean_energy) superpower.[^cite_note-270] In 2019, Quebec's electricity production amounted to 214 [terawatt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terawatt)-hours (TWh), 95% of which comes from hydroelectric power stations, and 4.7% of which come from [wind energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wind_energy). The public company [Hydro-Québec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hydro-Qu%C3%A9bec) occupies a dominant position in the production, transmission and distribution of electricity in Quebec. Hydro-Québec operates 63 hydroelectric power stations and 28 large reservoirs.[^cite_note-hydroquebec.com2-271] Because of the remoteness of Hydro-Québec's [TransÉnergie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hydro-Qu%C3%A9bec's_electricity_transmission_system) division, it operates the largest electricity transmission network in North America. Quebec stands out for its use of [renewable energy](25784.md). In 2008, electricity ranked as the main form of energy used in Quebec (41.6%), followed by [oil](4207510.md) (38.2%) and [natural gas](22131.md) (10.7%).[^cite_note-272] In 2017, 47% of all energy came from renewable sources.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhitmorePineau20208-273] The Quebec government's energy policy seeks to build, by 2030, a [low carbon economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Low_carbon_economy).

In 2011, the [mining industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mining_industry) accounted for 6.3% of Quebec's GDP[^cite_note-274] and it employed about 50,000 people in 158 companies.[^cite_note-275] It has around 30 mines, 158 exploration companies and 15 primary processing industries. While many metallic and industrial minerals are exploited, the main ones are gold, iron, copper and [zinc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zinc). Others include: [titanium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Titanium), [asbestos](21492663.md), silver, [magnesium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magnesium) and nickel, among many others.[^cite_note-276] Quebec is also as a major source of diamonds.[^cite_note-277] Since 2002, Quebec has seen an increase in its mineral explorations. In 2003, the value of mineral exploitation reached \$3.7 billion.[^cite_note-278]

The [agri-food industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agrifood) plays an important role in the economy of Quebec, with meat and dairy products being the two main sectors. It accounts for 8% of the Quebec's GDP and generate \$19.2 billion. In 2010, this industry generated 487,000 jobs in agriculture, fisheries, manufacturing of food, beverages and tobacco and food distribution.[^cite_note-279]

### Secondary sector



A mockup of the [Airbus A220](1567474.md) (formerly the Bombardier CSeries), originally developed by [Bombardier Aerospace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombardier_Aerospace)

In 2021, Quebec's [aerospace industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aerospace_industry) employed 35,000 people and its sales totalled C\$15.2 billion – the world's 6th largest.[^cite_note-280] Many aerospace companies are active in the country, including [CMC Electronics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CMC_Electronics), [Bombardier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombardier_Aerospace), [Pratt & Whitney Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pratt_&_Whitney_Canada), [Héroux-Devtek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/H%C3%A9roux-Devtek), [Rolls-Royce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rolls-Royce_Holdings), [General Electric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Electric), [Bell Textron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bell_Textron), [L3Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L3Harris), [Safran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safran), [SONACA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SONACA), [CAE Inc.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CAE_Inc.), and [Airbus](26220236.md), among others. Montreal is globally considered one of the aerospace industry's great centres, and several international aviation organisations seat here.[^cite_note-281] Both Aéro Montréal and the CRIAQ were created to assist aerospace companies.[^cite_note-282][^cite_note-283]

The [pulp and paper industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pulp_and_paper_industry) accounted for 3.1% of Quebec's GDP in 2007[^cite_note-284] and generated annual shipments valued at more than \$14 billion.[^cite_note-285] This industry employs 68,000 people in several regions of Quebec.[^cite_note-286] It is also the main—and in some circumstances only—source of manufacturing activity in more than 250 municipalities in the province. The forest industry has slowed in recent years because of the [softwood lumber dispute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_%E2%80%93_Canada_softwood_lumber_dispute).[^cite_note-287] In 2020, this industry represented 8% of Quebec's exports.[^cite_note-288]

As Quebec has few significant deposits of [fossil fuels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fossil_fuel),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhitmorePineau202010-289] all [hydrocarbons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hydrocarbon) are imported. Refiners' sourcing strategies have varied over time and have depended on market conditions. In the 1990s, Quebec purchased much of its oil from the [North Sea](1150532.md). Since 2015, it now consumes almost exclusively the crude produced in [western Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Canada) and the United States.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhitmorePineau20209-290] Quebec's two active refineries have a total capacity of 402,000 barrels per day, greater than local needs which stood at 365,000 barrels per day in 2018.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhitmorePineau202010-289]

By virtue of hydroelectricity, Quebec is the world's fourth largest [aluminum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aluminium_foil) producer and creates 90% of Canadian aluminum. Three companies make aluminum here: [Rio Tinto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rio_Tinto_\(corporation\)), [Alcoa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alcoa) and Aluminium Alouette. Their 9 *alumineries* produce 2,9 million tons of aluminum annually and employ 30,000 workers.[^cite_note-291]

### Tertiary sector

The [finance and insurance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_services) sector employs more than 168,000 people. Of this number, 78,000 are employed by the banking sector, 53,000 by the insurance sector and 20,000 by the securities and investment sector.[^cite_note-292] The [Bank of Montreal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bank_of_Montreal), founded in 1817 in Montreal, was Quebec's first bank but, like many other large banks, its central branch is now in Toronto. Several banks remain based in Quebec [National Bank of Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Bank_of_Canada), the [Desjardins Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Desjardins_Group) and the [Laurentian Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laurentian_Bank_of_Canada).



The [Château Frontenac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ch%C3%A2teau_Frontenac) is the most photographed hotel in the world.

The [tourism industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Quebec) is a major sector in Quebec. The [Ministry of Tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Tourism_\(Quebec\)) ensures the development of this industry under the commercial name "Bonjour Québec".[^cite_note-293] Quebec is the second most important province for tourism in Canada, receiving 21.5% of tourists' spending (2021).[^cite_note-294] The industry provides employment to over 400,000 people.[^cite_note-295] These employees work in the more than 29,000 tourism-related businesses in Quebec, most of which are restaurants or hotels. 70% of tourism-related businesses are located in or close to Montreal or Quebec City. It is estimated that, in 2010, Quebec welcomed 25.8 million tourists. Of these, 76.1% came from Quebec, 12.2% from the rest of Canada, 7.7% from the United States and 4.1% from other countries. Annually, tourists spend more than \$6.7 billion in Quebec's tourism industry.[^cite_note-296]

Approximately 1.1 million Quebecers work in the field of science and technology.[^cite_note-297] In 2007, the [Government of Quebec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Quebec) launched the Stratégie québécoise de la recherche et de l'innovation (SQRI) aiming to promote development through research, science and technology. The government hoped to create a strong culture of innovation in Quebec for the next decades and to create a [sustainable economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sustainable_economy).[^cite_note-298]

Quebec's [IT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Information_technology) sector has 7,600 businesses and employs 140,000 people.[^cite_note-299][^cite_note-300][^cite_note-301] Its most developed sectors are [telecommunications](33094374.md), multimedia and video game software, computer services, microelectronics, and the components sector. There are currently 115 telecommunications companies established in the province, including [Motorola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motorola), [Ericsson](9424.md) and Mitec.[^cite_note-302] The multimedia and video game sector has been growing fast since the early 2000s. The Digital Alliance, which claims 191 active members in video games, online education, mobility and Internet services, estimates the annual revenue of the sector at \$827 million in 2014.[^cite_note-303] The microelectronics sector is made up of more than 100 companies employing 13,000 people. Computer services, software development, and consulting engineering employ 60,000 skilled workers. While the largest IT employers are [CMC Electronics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CMC_Electronics), [IBM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IBM), and [Matrox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matrox), many other tech companies are present here, including [Ubisoft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ubisoft_Montreal), [Electronic Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronic_Arts), [Microids](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Microids), [Strategy First](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strategy_First), [Eidos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eidos-Montr%C3%A9al), [Activision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Activision), [A2M](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artificial_Mind_and_Movement), [Frima Studio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frima_Studio), etc.[^cite_note-304]

Montreal is ranked fourth in North America for the number of jobs in the pharmaceutical sector.[^cite_note-emergex2-305][^cite_note-306]

## Education

Main article: [Education in Quebec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Quebec)

See also: [List of universities in Quebec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_universities_in_Quebec), [List of colleges in Quebec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_colleges_in_Quebec), and [List of schools in Quebec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_schools_in_Quebec)



The [Institut national de la recherche scientifique](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institut_national_de_la_recherche_scientifique) helps to advance scientific knowledge and to train a new generation of students in various scientific and technological sectors.

The education system of Quebec, administered by the government of Quebec's [Ministry of Education and Higher Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Education_and_Higher_Education_\(Quebec\)), differs from those of other Canadian provinces. The province has five levels of education: first [preschool, then primary school, then secondary school](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Preschool,_primary_and_secondary_education_in_Quebec?action=edit\&redlink=1); then [CEGEP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CEGEP) (see [College education in Quebec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/College_education_in_Quebec)); and finally [university or college](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Higher_education_in_Quebec). Attached to these levels are the options to also attend professional development opportunities, [classes for adults](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andragogy), and [continuing education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Continuing_education). For every level of teaching, there exists a public network and private network: the public network is financed by taxes while the private options must be paid for by the student. In 2020, [school boards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/School_boards) were replaced by [school service centres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/School_service_centre).[^cite_note-307]

All universities in Quebec exist by virtue of laws adopted by the [National Assembly of Quebec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Assembly_of_Quebec) in 1967 during the [Quiet Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quiet_Revolution). Their financing mostly comes from public taxes, but the laws under which they operate grants them more autonomy than other levels of education.[^cite_note-308]

### Research



In 1969, [Héroux-Devtek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/H%C3%A9roux-Devtek) designed and manufactured the [undercarriage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Landing_gear) of the [Apollo Lunar Module](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollo_Lunar_Module).

Quebec is considered one of world leaders in [fundamental scientific research](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basic_research), having produced ten [Nobel laureates](21201.md) in either physics, chemistry, or medicine.[^cite_note-309] It is also considered one of the world leaders in sectors such as aerospace, information technology, biotechnology and pharmaceuticals, and therefore plays a significant role in the world's scientific and technological communities.[^cite_note-310] Between 2000 and 2011, Quebec had over 9,469 scientific publications in biomedical research and engineering.[^cite_note-311] The contribution of Quebec in science and technology represented approximately 1% of the research worldwide between the 1980s and 2009.[^cite_note-312]

The province is one of the world leaders in the field of [space science](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_science) and contributed to important discoveries in this field.[^cite_note-313] The [Canadian Space Agency](328618.md) was established in Quebec due to its major role in this research field. A total of four Quebecers have been in space since the creation of the CSA: [Marc Garneau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marc_Garneau), [Julie Payette](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julie_Payette), and [David Saint-Jacques](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Saint-Jacques) as CSA astronauts, plus [Guy Laliberté](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guy_Lalibert%C3%A9) as a private citizen who paid for his trip. Quebec has also contributed to the creation of some Canadian [artificial satellites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artificial_satellite) including [SCISAT-1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SCISAT-1), [ISIS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISIS_\(satellite\)), [Radarsat-1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radarsat-1) and [Radarsat-2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radarsat-2).[^cite_note-314][^cite_note-315][^cite_note-316]

Quebec ranks among the world leaders in the field of [life science](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Life_science).[^cite_note-emergex2-305] Quebec has more than 450 biotechnology and pharmaceutical companies which together employ more than 25,000 people and 10,000 highly qualified researchers.[^cite_note-emergex2-305]

## Infrastructure

### Transport

Main articles: [List of airports in Quebec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_airports_in_Quebec), [List of Quebec railways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Quebec_railways), and [List of Quebec provincial highways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Quebec_provincial_highways)



The ferry N.M. *Camille-Marcoux*, of the *Société des traversiers du Québec*

Land transport development and security are provided by [Transports Québec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transports_Qu%C3%A9bec).[^cite_note-317] [Société de l'assurance automobile du Québec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soci%C3%A9t%C3%A9_de_l'assurance_automobile_du_Qu%C3%A9bec) (SAAQ; Quebec Automobile Insurance Corporation) manages the *réseau routier québécois* (Quebec road network), which consists of about 185,000 km (115,000 mi) of highways and national, regional, local, collector and forest roads.&#x20;

[Canadian Coast Guard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Coast_Guard) manages maritime transport. The Saint Lawrence hosts eight deep-water ports. In 2003, 3,886 cargo ships and 9.7 million tonnes of goods transited the Quebec portion of the [Saint Lawrence Seaway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Lawrence_Seaway).[^cite_note-transport-qc2-318]

The *Commission des transports du Québec* works with freight carriers and [public transport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_transport). Quebec has 6,678 km (4,150 mi) of railways[^cite_note-319] integrated into the North American network. Although primarily intended for goods transport through carriers such as [Canadian National](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_National) (CN) and the [Canadian Pacific](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Pacific) (CP), the Quebec railway network is also used by inter-city passengers via [Via Rail Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Via_Rail_Canada) and [Amtrak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amtrak).[^cite_note-320]

[Nav Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nav_Canada) manages air transport. Quebec's air network includes 43 airports that offer daily scheduled services.[^cite_note-transport-qc2-318] In addition, the Government of Quebec owns airports and heliports in the [Basse-Côte-Nord](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basse-C%C3%B4te-Nord) and northern regions.[^cite_note-321]

Quebec has almost 12,000 bridges, tunnels, retaining walls, culverts and other structures[^cite_note-322] such as the [Quebec Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quebec_Bridge), the [Laviolette Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laviolette_Bridge) and the [Louis-Hippolyte Lafontaine Bridge–Tunnel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis-Hippolyte_Lafontaine_Bridge%E2%80%93Tunnel).

Other transport networks crisscross the province, including [hiking trails](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trail), [snowmobile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Snowmobile) trails and bike paths. The [Green Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Route_Verte) is the largest at nearly 4,000 km (2,500 mi) in length.[^cite_note-323]

### Healthcare

Quebec's health policy emphasizes prevention. Similar to other developed economies, life expectancy has lengthened beginning in the 20th century.[^cite_note-324]

Health and social services are provided by one organization. The Quebec health system is public, which means that the government acts as the main insurer and administrator, funding care with taxation, and that patients have access to care regardless of income.

34 health establishments operate in Quebec, 22 via an [Integrated Health and Social Services Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Integrated_Health_and_Social_Services_Centre?action=edit\&redlink=1) (CISSS). Quebec has approximately 140 hospitals for general or specialised care (CHSGS). Other types of establishments include *Centre local de services communautaires* (CLSC), *Centre d'hébergement et de soins de longue durée* (CHSLD), *Centre de réadaptation* and *Centre de protection de l'enfance et de la jeunesse*. Private healthcare establishments (paid directly by the patient) include [Groupe de médecine de famille](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Groupe_de_m%C3%A9decine_de_famille?action=edit\&redlink=1), pharmacies, private clinics, dentists, community organisations, and retirement homes.[^cite_note-325]

A 2021 Ipsos poll reported that 85% of Quebecers agree that their health care system is too bureaucratic[^cite_note-326] and in 2023 that fewer than half of Quebecers were satisfied with the system.[^cite_note-327]

### Housing

Main article: [Housing crisis in Quebec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Housing_crisis_in_Quebec)

In 2021, 59.9% of Quebec's residents were property owners.[^cite_note-328] In 2019, among property owners, 34% were couples with kids, 33% were couples without children, 22% lived alone, 8% were single parents, and 3% were something else. Among renters, 16% were couples with kids, 13% were couples without children, 51% lived alone, 13% were single parents, and 7% were something else.[^cite_note-habitation-217]

Since the 1980s, the average price of a single-family home has doubled every 10 years, going from \$48,715 in 1980 to \$424,844 in 2021. Since the average salary did not follow these increases, Quebec homes are 10 times more expensive than they were 40 years ago.[^cite_note-329] In 2022, the cities with the most severe housing shortages were [Granby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Granby,_Quebec), with a vacancy rate of 0,1%, followed by [Marieville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marieville) (0,1%), [Rimouski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rimouski) (0,2%), [Drummondville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drummondville) (0,2%) and [Rouyn-Noranda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rouyn-Noranda) (0,3%).[^cite_note-330]

## Culture

Main article: [Culture of Quebec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Quebec)

Quebec has developed its own unique culture from its historic [New France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_France) roots. Its culture also symbolizes a distinct perspective: being a French-speaking nation surrounded by a bigger English-speaking culture.

The [Quartier Latin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quartier_Latin,_Montreal) (English: Latin Quarter) of Montreal, and Vieux-Québec (English: Old Quebec) in Quebec City are two hubs of metropolitan cultural activity. Life in the cafés and "terrasses" (outdoor restaurant terraces) reveals a Latin influence in Quebec's culture, with the [théâtre Saint-Denis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Th%C3%A9%C3%A2tre_Saint-Denis) in Montreal and the [Capitole de Québec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capitole_de_Qu%C3%A9bec) theatre in Quebec City being among the principal attractions.

A number of governmental and non-government organizations support cultural activity in Quebec. The [Conseil des arts et des lettres du Québec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conseil_des_arts_et_des_lettres_du_Qu%C3%A9bec) (CALQ) is an initiative of the [Ministry of Culture and Communications (Quebec)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Culture_and_Communications_\(Quebec\)). It supports creation, innovation, production, and international exhibits for all cultural fields of Quebec. The [Société de développement des entreprises culturelles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soci%C3%A9t%C3%A9_de_d%C3%A9veloppement_des_entreprises_culturelles) (SODEC) works to promote and fund individuals working in the cultural industry. The [Prix du Québec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prix_du_Qu%C3%A9bec) is an award given by the government to confer the highest distinction and honour to individuals demonstrating exceptional achievement in their respective cultural field. Other awards include the Athanase David Awards (Literature), [Félix Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F%C3%A9lix_Awards) (Music), [Gémeaux Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G%C3%A9meaux_Awards) (Television and film), [Jutra Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jutra_Awards) (Cinema), Masques Awards (Theatre), [Olivier Guimond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olivier_Guimond) Awards (Humour) and the Opus Awards (Concert music).

### Performing arts

Main articles: [Music of Quebec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_Quebec), [Dance in Quebec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dance_in_Quebec), and [Comedy in Quebec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comedy_in_Quebec)

Traditional music is imbued with many dances, such as the [jig](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jig), the [quadrille](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quadrille), the [reel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reel_\(dance\)) and [line dancing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Line_dancing). Traditional instruments include harmonica, [fiddle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fiddle), [spoons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spoon_\(musical_instrument\)), [jaw harp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaw_harp) and [accordion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Accordion). The [First Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Nations_in_Canada) and the [Inuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inuit) of Quebec also have their own traditional music. Quebec's most popular artists of the last century include the singers [Félix Leclerc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F%C3%A9lix_Leclerc), [Gilles Vigneault](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gilles_Vigneault), [Kate and Anna McGarrigle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kate_and_Anna_McGarrigle) and [Céline Dion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C%C3%A9line_Dion).[^cite_note-331] The [Association québécoise de l'industrie du disque, du spectacle et de la vidéo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ADISQ) (ADISQ) was created in 1978 to promote the music industry in Quebec.[^cite_note-332] The [Orchestre symphonique de Québec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orchestre_symphonique_de_Qu%C3%A9bec) and the [Montreal Symphony Orchestra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montreal_Symphony_Orchestra) are respectively associated with the Opéra de Québec and the Opéra de Montreal, whose performances are presented at the [Grand Théâtre de Québec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Th%C3%A9%C3%A2tre_de_Qu%C3%A9bec) and at [Place des Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Place_des_Arts). The Ballets Jazz de Montreal, the Grands Ballets and [La La La Human Steps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_La_La_Human_Steps) are three important professional troupes of [contemporary dance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contemporary_dance).

Among the theatre troupes are the [Compagnie Jean-Duceppe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Compagnie_Jean-Duceppe), the Théâtre La Rubrique, and the Théâtre Le Grenier. In addition to the network of cultural centres in Quebec,[^cite_note-333] the venues include the [Monument-National](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monument-National) and the Rideau Vert (green curtain) Theatre in Montreal, and the *Trident* Theatre in Quebec City. The [National Theatre School of Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Theatre_School_of_Canada) and the [Conservatoire de musique et d'art dramatique du Québec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservatoire_de_musique_et_d'art_dramatique_du_Qu%C3%A9bec) train actors.



The show *Dralion*, Cirque du Soleil, introduced in 2004

Several circus troupes were created in recent decades, the most important being [Cirque du Soleil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cirque_du_Soleil).[^cite_note-334] Among these troops are contemporary, travelling and on-horseback circuses, such as [Les 7 Doigts de la Main](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Les_7_Doigts_de_la_Main?action=edit\&redlink=1), [Cirque Éloize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cirque_%C3%89loize), [Cavalia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cavalia), Kosmogonia, Saka and Cirque Akya.[^cite_note-335] The [National Circus School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89cole_nationale_de_cirque) and the École de cirque de Québec were created to train [contemporary circus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contemporary_circus) artists. [Tohu, la Cité des Arts du Cirque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tohu,_la_Cit%C3%A9_des_Arts_du_Cirque?action=edit\&redlink=1), was founded in 2004 to disseminate the circus arts.[^cite_note-336]

Comedy is a vast [cultural sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comedy_in_Quebec). Quebec has created and is home to several different comedy festivals, including the [Just for Laughs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Just_for_Laughs) festival in Montreal, as well as the Grand Rire festivals of Quebec, Gatineau and Sherbrooke.[^cite_note-337] The Association des professionnels de l'industrie de l'humour (APIH) is the main organization for the promotion and development of the cultural sector of humour in Quebec and the [National School of Humour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_School_of_Humour?action=edit\&redlink=1), created in 1988, trains humorists in Quebec.

### Media

Main articles: [Cinema of Quebec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinema_of_Quebec) and [Television in Quebec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Television_in_Quebec)

The [Cinémathèque québécoise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cin%C3%A9math%C3%A8que_qu%C3%A9b%C3%A9coise) has a mandate to promote the film and television heritage of Quebec. The [National Film Board of Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Film_Board_of_Canada) (NFB), a federal Crown corporation, provides for the same mission in Canada. The Association of Film and Television in Quebec (APFTQ) promotes independent production in film and television.[^cite_note-338] While the Association of Producers and Directors of Quebec (APDQ) represents the business of filmmaking and television, the Association of Community Radio Broadcasters of Quebec (ARCQ) (French acronym) represents the independent radio stations.[^cite_note-339] Several movie theatres across Quebec ensure the dissemination of Quebec cinema. With its cinematic installations, such as the *Cité du cinéma* and *Mel's* studios, the city of Montreal is home to the filming of various productions.[^cite_note-340] The state corporation [Télé-Québec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T%C3%A9l%C3%A9-Qu%C3%A9bec), the federal Crown corporation [CBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Broadcasting_Corporation), general and specialized private channels, networks, independent and community radio stations broadcast the various [Quebec téléromans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T%C3%A9l%C3%A9roman), the national and regional news, and other programming.[^cite_note-341][^cite_note-342] [Les Rendez-vous du cinéma québécois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Les_Rendez-vous_du_cin%C3%A9ma_qu%C3%A9b%C3%A9cois) is a festival surrounding the ceremony of the [Jutra Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jutra_Award) Night that rewards work and personalities of Quebec cinema.[^cite_note-343] The Artis and the [Gemini Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gemini_Awards) gala recognize the personalities of television and radio industry in Quebec and French Canada. The *Film Festival of the 3 Americas*, the *Festival of International Short Film*, the [World Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montreal_World_Film_Festival) and the [Festival of New Cinema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Festival_du_Nouveau_Cin%C3%A9ma) are other annual events surrounding the film industry in Quebec.

In the realm of literature and international publishing, the *Québec Édition* group is a committee created by the National Association of Book Editors dedicated to the international influence of French-language publishings from Quebec and Canada.[^cite_note-344]

### Literature and folklore

Main articles: [Quebec literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quebec_literature) and [Folklore of Quebec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folklore_of_Quebec)



*La [chasse-galerie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chasse-galerie)* (1906) by [Henri Julien](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henri_Julien), showing a scene from a popular Quebec folk legend

Quebec's French-speaking populace has the second largest body of folktales in Canada (the first being [First Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Nations_in_Canada)).[^cite_note-345] When the early settlers arrived from France in the 17th century, they brought with them popular tales from their homeland, which were adapted to the local context. Many were passed on through generations by *raconteurs*, or storytellers.[^cite_note-346] Almost all of the stories native to Quebec were influenced by Christian dogma and superstitions. The [Devil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Devil), for instance, appears often as either a person, an animal or monster, or indirectly through Demonic acts.[^cite_note-Chiasson2-347] Various tales and stories are told through oral tradition, such as, among many others, the legends of the *[Bogeyman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bogeyman)*, the *[Chasse-galerie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chasse-galerie)*, the *Black Horse of Trois-Pistoles*, the *Complainte de Cadieux*, the *[Corriveau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marie-Josephte_Corriveau)*, the *dancing devil of Saint-Ambroise*, the *[Giant Beaupré](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89douard_Beaupr%C3%A9)*, the *monsters of the lakes [Pohénégamook](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Pohenegamook)* and *[Memphremagog](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Memphremagog)*, of *[Quebec Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quebec_Bridge)* (called the Devil's Bridge), the *[Rocher Percé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rocher_Perc%C3%A9)* and of *Rose Latulipe*, for example.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDupont2008-348]

From New France, Quebec literature was first developed in the travel accounts of explorers. The *Moulin à paroles* traces the great texts that have shaped the history of Quebec. The first to write the history of Quebec, since its discovery, was the historian [François-Xavier Garneau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fran%C3%A7ois-Xavier_Garneau). Many [Quebec poets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Quebec_writers) and prominent authors marked their era and today remain anchored in the collective imagination, like, among others, Philippe Aubert de Gaspé, [Octave Crémazie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Octave_Cr%C3%A9mazie), [Honoré Beaugrand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honor%C3%A9_Beaugrand), [Émile Nelligan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89mile_Nelligan), [Lionel Groulx](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lionel_Groulx), [Gabrielle Roy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gabrielle_Roy), [Hubert Aquin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hubert_Aquin), [Michel Tremblay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michel_Tremblay), [Marie Laberge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marie_Laberge), Fred Pellerin and [Gaston Miron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaston_Miron). The [regional novel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Literary_Regionalism) from Quebec is called *Terroir* novel and is a literary tradition[^cite_note-349] specific to the province.

### Art and architecture

Main article: [Architecture of Quebec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Architecture_of_Quebec)

See also: [List of art schools in Quebec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_art_schools_in_Quebec)



*La Cavalière* by [Charles Daudelin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Daudelin), 1963, installed in front of the pavilion Gérard Morisset of the Quebec National Museum of Fine Arts in [Quebec City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quebec_City)

The art of Quebec has developed around the specific characteristics of its landscapes and cultural, historical, social and political representations. The development of Quebec masterpieces in painting, printmaking and sculpture is marked by the contribution of artists such as [Louis-Philippe Hébert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis-Philippe_H%C3%A9bert), [Cornelius Krieghoff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cornelius_Krieghoff), [Alfred Laliberté](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alfred_Lalibert%C3%A9), [Marc-Aurèle Fortin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marc-Aur%C3%A8le_Fortin), [Marc-Aurèle de Foy Suzor-Coté](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marc-Aur%C3%A8le_de_Foy_Suzor-Cot%C3%A9), [Jean Paul Lemieux](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean_Paul_Lemieux), [Clarence Gagnon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clarence_Gagnon), Adrien Dufresne, [Alfred Pellan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alfred_Pellan), [Jean-Philippe Dallaire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean-Philippe_Dallaire), [Charles Daudelin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Daudelin), [Arthur Villeneuve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arthur_Villeneuve), [Jean-Paul Riopelle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean-Paul_Riopelle), [Paul-Émile Borduas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul-%C3%89mile_Borduas) and [Marcelle Ferron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcelle_Ferron).

The fine arts of Quebec are displayed at the [Quebec National Museum of Fine Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mus%C3%A9e_national_des_beaux-arts_du_Qu%C3%A9bec), the [Montreal Museum of Contemporary Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mus%C3%A9e_d'art_contemporain_de_Montr%C3%A9al), the [Montreal Museum of Fine Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montreal_Museum_of_Fine_Arts), the Quebec *Salon des métiers d'art* and in many art galleries. The [Montreal School of Fine Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89cole_des_Beaux-Arts_de_Montr%C3%A9al) forms the [painters, printmakers and sculptors of Quebec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Canadian_artists).



*Maison Routhier* in [Sainte-Foy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sainte-Foy,_Quebec_City), a [Canadien](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadien)-style house

Quebec's architecture is characterized by its unique [Canadien](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadien)-style buildings as well as the juxtaposition of a variety of styles reflective of Quebec's history. When walking in any city or town, one can come across buildings with styles congruent to [Classical](52687.md), [Neo-Gothic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neo-Gothic), [Roman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_architecture), [Neo-Renaissance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neo-Renaissance), [Greek Revival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek_Revival_architecture), [Neo-Classical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neo-classical_architecture), Québécois Neo-Classical, [Victorian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victorian_architecture), [Second Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Empire_architecture), [Modern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modern_architecture), [Post-modern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Post-modern_architecture) or skyscrapers.

Canadien-style houses and barns were developed by the first settlers of New France along the banks of the Saint Lawrence River. These buildings are rectangular one-storey structures with an extremely tall and steep roof, sometimes almost twice as tall as the house below. Canadien-style churches also developed and served as landmarks while traversing rural Quebec.

### Heritage

Main articles: [Quebec Cultural Heritage Directory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quebec_Cultural_Heritage_Directory) and [Bibliothèque et Archives nationales du Québec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biblioth%C3%A8que_et_Archives_nationales_du_Qu%C3%A9bec)

See also: [List of museums in Quebec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_museums_in_Quebec)



[Johan Beetz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johan_Beetz) House,

[^cite_note-Johan_Beetz_House,_heritage-350]

&#x20;a [Second Empire-inspired](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Empire_style) rural residence at [Baie-Johan-Beetz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baie-Johan-Beetz), [Minganie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minganie_Regional_County_Municipality)

Several sites, houses and historical works reflect the cultural heritage of Quebec, such as the [Village Québécois d'Antan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Village_Qu%C3%A9b%C3%A9cois_d'Antan), the historical village of [Val-Jalbert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Val-Jalbert), the [Fort Chambly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Chambly), the national home of the Patriots, the Chicoutimi pulp mill (Pulperie de Chicoutimi), the [Lachine Canal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lachine_Canal) and the [Victoria Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_Bridge_\(Montreal\)). As of December 2011, there are 198 [National Historic Sites of Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_National_Historic_Sites_of_Canada_in_Quebec) in Quebec.[^cite_note-Explore_Quebec,_Parc_Canada-351] These sites were designated as being of national historic significance.[^cite_note-352]

Various museums tell the cultural history of Quebec, like the [Museum of Civilization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mus%C3%A9e_de_la_civilisation), the [Museum of French America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mus%C3%A9e_de_l'Am%C3%A9rique_fran%C3%A7aise), the [McCord Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McCord_Museum) or the Montreal Museum of Archaeology and History in [Pointe-à-Callière](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pointe-%C3%A0-Calli%C3%A8re), displaying artifacts, paintings and other remains from the past of Quebec.
