Notable schools include the [Conservatoire de musique et d'art dramatique du Québec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservatoire_de_musique_et_d'art_dramatique_du_Qu%C3%A9bec), the [École nationale de théâtre du Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Theatre_School_of_Canada) and the [École nationale de cirque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89cole_nationale_de_cirque). Notable public agencies to catalogue and further develop Quebec's culture include the [Bibliothèque et Archives nationales du Québec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biblioth%C3%A8que_et_Archives_nationales_du_Qu%C3%A9bec), the [Conseil des arts et des lettres du Québec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conseil_des_arts_et_des_lettres_du_Qu%C3%A9bec) and [Télé-Québec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T%C3%A9l%C3%A9-Qu%C3%A9bec). The *Association québécoise des loisirs folkloriques* is an organization committed to preserving and disseminating Quebec's folklore heritage.[^cite_note-353]

### Cuisine

Main article: [Cuisine of Quebec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuisine_of_Quebec)



A classic [poutine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poutine)

The traditional Quebecois cuisine descends from 16th-century [French cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_cuisine), the [fur trade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fur_trade) and a history of [hunting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hunting). Quebec's cuisine has also been influenced by learning from [First Nation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Nations_in_Canada), by [English cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_cuisine) and by [American cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_cuisine). Quebec is most famous for its [tourtière](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourti%C3%A8re), [pâté chinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/P%C3%A2t%C3%A9_chinois), [poutine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poutine), and [St. Catherine's taffy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Catherine's_taffy) among others. The "*temps des sucres*" is a period during springtime when many Quebecers go to the [sugar shack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sugar_shack) (*cabane à sucre*) for a traditional meal.

Quebec is the world's biggest [maple syrup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maple_syrup) producer.[^cite_note-bbc17-354] The province has a long history of producing maple syrup, and creating new [maple-derived products](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_foods_made_from_maple). Other major food products include beer, wine (including [ice wine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ice_wine) and [ice cider](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ice_cider)), and cheese.

### Sports



The [Montreal Canadiens](42966.md) at the [Bell Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bell_Centre)

Sports in Quebec constitutes an essential dimension of Quebec culture. [Ice hockey](14790.md) remains the national sport. This sport was played for the first time on March 3, 1875, in Montreal and has been promoted over the years by numerous achievements, including the centenary of the [Montreal Canadiens](42966.md).[^cite_note-355] Other major sports include [Canadian football](6963.md) with the [Montreal Alouettes](70601.md), soccer with [Club de Foot Montréal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Club_de_Foot_Montr%C3%A9al), the [Grand Prix du Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Grand_Prix) Formula 1 racing with drivers such as [Gilles Villeneuve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gilles_Villeneuve) and [Jacques Villeneuve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacques_Villeneuve), and professional baseball with the former [Montreal Expos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montreal_Expos). Quebec has hosted several major sporting events, including the [1976 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1976_Summer_Olympics), the Fencing World Championships in 1967, track cycling in 1974, and the [Transat Québec-Saint-Malo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transat_Qu%C3%A9bec-Saint-Malo) race created in 1984.

Quebec athletes have an above-average performance at the [Winter Olympics](34069.md) over recent years. The athletic team was collectively awarded 12 of 29 medals won by Canada at the [2018 Winter Olympics in Pyeongchang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Winter_Olympics), 12 of the 27 Canadian medals in [2014](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Winter_Olympics), and 9 of the 26 Canadian medals in [2010](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Winter_Olympics).[^cite_note-356]

### Holidays and symbols

Main article: [Symbols of Quebec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Symbols_of_Quebec)

[St-Jean-Baptiste Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St-Jean-Baptiste_Day) is one of Quebec's biggest holidays. In 1977, the [Quebec Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quebec_Parliament) declared June 24, the day of *La Saint-Jean-Baptiste*, to be [Quebec's National Holiday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quebec's_National_Holiday). *La Saint-Jean-Baptiste*, or *La St-Jean*, honours [French Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Canada)'s patron saint, [John the Baptist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_the_Baptist). On this day, the song "[Gens du pays](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gens_du_pays)", by [Gilles Vigneault](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gilles_Vigneault), is often heard. The song *[À la claire fontaine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%80_la_claire_fontaine)*[^cite_note-357] was the anthem of the [New France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_France), [Patriots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patriote_movement) and [French Canadian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Canadian), then replaced by *[O Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/O_Canada)*, but "Gens du pays" is preferred by many Quebecers to be the national anthem of Quebec.

[National Patriots' Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Patriots'_Day), a statutory holiday in Quebec, is also a unique public holiday, which honours the patriotes with displays of the [patriote flag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patriote_flag), music, public speeches, and ceremonies.[^cite_note-law2-358] *[Le Vieux de '37](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Le_Vieux_de_'37)* ("The Old Man of '37"), an illustration by [Henri Julien](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henri_Julien) that depicts a patriot of this rebellion,[^cite_note-359] is sometimes added at the centre of [Patriote flags](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patriote_flags). [Moving Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moving_Day_\(Quebec\)) is a tradition where leases terminate on July 1. This creates a social phenomenon where everyone seems to be moving out at the same time.[^cite_note-360]

Other distinct holiday traditions include the [Réveillon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R%C3%A9veillon), a giant feast and party which takes place during [Christmas Eve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christmas_Eve) and [New Year's Eve](277922.md) and goes on until midnight. Traditional dishes like [tourtière](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourti%C3%A8re) or [cipâte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sea-pie) are offered, and [rigaudon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rigaudon), [spoon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spoon_\(musical_instrument\)) or [violin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Violin) may be played.[^cite_note-361] Finally, [April Fools' Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/April_Fools'_Day) is called *Poisson d'Avril* ("April's Fish") because while pulling pranks is still important, there is another major tradition: sticking fish-shaped paper cutouts to people's backs without them noticing.[^cite_note-362]

In 1939, the [government of Quebec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Quebec) unilaterally ratified its [coat of arms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coat_of_arms_of_Quebec) to reflect [Quebec's political history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Quebec): French rule (gold lily on blue background), followed by British rule (lion on red background), followed by Canadian rule (maple leaves).[^cite_note-363] [Je me souviens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Je_me_souviens) ("I remember") is an official part of the coat of arms and has been the official licence plate motto since 1978, replacing the previous motto: *La belle province* ("the beautiful province"), still used as a nickname for the province. The [fleur-de-lis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fleur-de-lis), one of Quebec's most common symbols, is an ancient symbol of the [French monarchy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_monarchy). Finally, the [Great Seal of Quebec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Seal_of_Quebec) is used to authenticate documents issued by the government of Quebec.

&#x20;The first members of the [Saint-Jean-Baptiste Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint-Jean-Baptiste_Society) created the *Carillon Sacré-Coeur* flag, which consisted of a white cross on an azure background with white fleur-de-lis in each corner and a [Sacred Heart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sacred_Heart#Sacred_Heart_imagery) surrounded by [maple leaves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maple_leaf) in the centre; it was based on the French merchant flag flown by Champlain and the [Flag of Carillon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flag_of_Carillon). The *Carillon Sacré-Coeur* and French merchant flag to be the major inspirations for creating Quebec's current flag in 1903, called the *[Fleurdelisé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fleurdelis%C3%A9)*. The *Fleurdelisé* replaced the [Union Jack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_Jack) on Quebec's [Parliament Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_Building_\(Quebec\)) on January 21, 1948.



*[Iris versicolor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iris_versicolor)* – [floral emblem of Quebec](#Emblems_of_Quebec)

Three new official emblems in were adopted in the late 20th century: the [snowy owl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Snowy_owl) in 1987 to symbolize the whiteness of Quebec's semi-northern climate, the [yellow birch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Betula_alleghaniensis) in 1993 for the variety of its uses and by its commercial value, and the [iris versicolor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iris_versicolor) in 1999 to illustrate the cultural diversity of Quebec and the importance of water and wetlands for the balance of nature.[^cite_note-Qsymbols2-364][^cite_note-Emblems_of_Quebec-365]

## Quebec's diaspora

Main article: [Quebec diaspora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quebec_diaspora)

The earliest immigrants to the [Canadian prairies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_prairies) were [French Canadians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Canadians) from Quebec. Many [Franco-Albertans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franco-Albertans), [Fransaskois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fransaskois) and [Franco-Manitobans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franco-Manitobans) are descended from them.

From the mid-1800s to the [Great Depression](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Depression), Quebec experienced the [Grande Hémorragie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grande_H%C3%A9morragie) ("Great Hemorrhaging"), a massive emigration of 900,000 people from Quebec to [New England](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_England).[^cite_note-366] French Canadians often established themselves in [Little Canadas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Little_Canadas) in many industrial New England centres. Of the 900,000 Québécois who emigrated, about half returned at some point before settling permanently.[^cite_note-367] Most of the descendants of those who stayed are now assimilated, though a few [Franco-Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franco-Americans) remain, speaking [New England French](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_England_French).

Some tried to slow the Grande Hémorragie by redirecting people north, which resulted in the founding of many regions in Quebec (ex. [Saguenay-Lac-St-Jean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saguenay-Lac-St-Jean), [Val-d'Or](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Val-d'Or)) but also in [Northeastern Ontario](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northeastern_Ontario). The northeastern [Franco-Ontarians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franco-Ontarian) of today, who live in [Timmins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timmins), [Hearst](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hearst,_Ontario), [Moosonee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moosonee) and [Sault Sainte Marie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sault_Ste._Marie,_Ontario), among others, are the descendants of emigrants from Quebec who worked in the mines of the area.[^cite_note-368]

In recent times, [snowbirds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Snowbird_\(person\)) often migrate to southern [Florida](18933066.md) during the winter, resulting in the emergence of temporary "Québécois regions," such as in [Hollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood,_Florida).[^cite_note-369]
